2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 隨著法國距離下屆總統大選僅剩六個月，極左翼與政府間的政治對立升溫，然而
法國政府急需推動預算案以控制財政赤字與龐大債務。在內政危機加劇與工會
預告 11月大罷工的背景下，市場對法國債務可持續性的擔憂升溫，將帶動法德
公債利差維持高檔，進而推升歐元區主權風險溢酬並拖累歐元表現。
 上週路透最新調查顯示，在 100 位受訪經濟學家中，高達 94%預期聯準會將於
10 月會議維持利率在 3.75%-4.00% 不變，惟全員一致預估 12 月將重啟升息。
儘管美國受 AI 投資熱潮帶動、經濟成長強勁，且美伊戰事推升油價突破百元
大關、加徵關稅使 PCE 通膨至少在 2028 年前難以回到 2% 目標，官員仍傾
向審慎行事。不過逾四成專家警告，聯準會恐落後通膨形勢，後續面臨至少再升
息兩次甚至擴大升息幅度的上行風險，預期美元大幅回檔的空間小，逢低承接買
盤依然強勁。
 路透最新調查顯示，受日圓疲軟與中東戰事推升能源及進口成本、加劇通膨壓力

影響，近七成受訪經濟學家預計日本央行將於 12月再度升息至 1.50%，延續 6
月與 9 月以來的較快緊縮步伐；同時，逾半數專家預估終端利率將上看 2.00%
或更高。不過，高達近四分之三受訪者警告，若高市早苗內閣提名的鴿派委員再
次投下反對票，恐壓抑日圓表現。在歐美央行年底前亦可能升息的背景下，日本
央行維持較快的緊縮節奏，有助於防止美日、歐日利差進一步擴大，避免日圓在
年底遭遇新一輪劇烈拋售，為日圓提供實質的下檔保護。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.360，低點在 32.704，以 32.508作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，台幣跳空開低，跌破今
年 7 月的低點 32.528；隨著台股表現不佳，外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣續創低位，
最低來到 32.704周內低點；而後台股低檔反彈，外資由匯出轉為雙向進出，加以出
口商積極進場拋售美金，台幣由貶轉升至 32.500下方；臨近周末，市場擔憂颱風來
襲周五可能休市，增添連續假期變數，外資再度大幅賣超台股並匯出，令台幣又跌
回 32.600上方，終場接在央行調節之下收在 32.508。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。近期全球科技股因 AI 類股估值偏高及融資風險疑慮
而持續修正，市場風險偏好降溫，股市面臨修正，外資連續賣超台股並匯出，台幣
走勢持續面臨壓力。另一方面，美伊戰事和談進展的消息反覆，令國際油價節節攀
升，突破每桶 110美元上方，引發通膨升溫的擔憂，支撐利率維持在高檔的預期，
美元指數也在 101.5 上方高位震盪，將不利台幣的表現。盤勢上來看，外資隨著股
市回檔修正表現偏向賣超台股並匯出，台幣偏弱，不過本周適逢月底，預計匯價逢
高仍持續有出口商拋售美金的需求，將限制台幣走貶的幅度。綜合以上，預估本週
台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走升。周初中國公布9月工業生產物價指數 5.5%，表現優於預期的
5.4%(前值 5.2%)，且第三季 GDP表現 4.5%，也優於預期的 4.4%(前值 4.3%)，基本
面穩健支撐人民幣升破 6.6800 整數關口；不過周間美元指數持續於 101.5 上方高位
震盪，加以美伊戰事未見緩解，國際油價持續飆升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，人民
幣於 6.6800下方高檔震盪；臨近周末，人行中間價格連續走升，升破6.7200整數關
口，週五最高來到 6.7180，顯示中國人行允許人民幣溫和升值的走勢，終場境外人
民幣升破 6.6700整數關口 ， 收在6.6690， 週線升值160點 ， 境內人民幣則收在6.6704，
週線升值 166 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價持續偏升，將持續帶
動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現；就國際市場來看，最
新利率期貨顯示 ， 本周聯準會利率決議有9成的機率維持利率在 3.75%~4.00%不變 ，
不過年底前仍有一碼的升息空間，加以地緣政治風險未見緩解，能源價格高檔震盪
推升通膨與利率預期，美元指數維持在 101.5 上方高檔震盪，將限制人民幣升值的
速度跟幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價
維持在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6300-6.6900

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌。週初，USD/JPY 大致在 158 的整數關卡上下波動；
週間，AI相關概念股遭拋售，加之中東局勢緊張推升避險需求，10年期美債殖利率
跌破 5%，避險資金亦流入日圓，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來至 156.67；臨近週末，
市場避險需求持續加之日本 9 月通膨數據高於日本央行 2%目標，USD/JPY 一度下
跌至 156.06，後逢低買盤進場跌幅有所收斂，終場收在 156.86，週線下跌 113點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下： 一、美國10 月 S&P製造業 PMI
為 56.0高於 9 月的 55.9且為 53個月高點，服務業則為 57.9低於 9月的 58.8，綜合
PMI為 57.6亦低於 9 月的 58.4，顯示美國商業活動雖略有放緩但仍處於擴張，經濟
基本面依然穩健將持續支撐美元；此外，中東局勢惡化使能源供應風險上升，布蘭
特原油價格突破 116美元，為六個月以來高點，也將有利USD/JPY上行。二、日本
9 月全國整體消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.1%，且 9 月核心 CPI年增 2.1%，為九
個月以來首次超過日本央行 2%的目標，強化市場對日本央行短期升息的預期，預計
為日圓帶來支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY匯價處於各天期均線之
下且接連跌破 158 及 157 關卡，後在 156 整數關卡暫時獲得支撐，顯示逢低仍有買
盤進場，將限縮下行幅度。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較
高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.06 158.59

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD震盪下跌。週初，EUR/USD在 1.1200整數關卡窄幅波動；週間
受到油價及美債殖利率回落，外加法國社會局勢略見緩解影響，帶動匯價反彈至
1.1260遇到賣壓後回落，後續大致在1.1190至 1.1250間波動；臨近週末，歐元區10
月份 PMI表現低迷，且市場對於法國政局和財政赤字擔憂持續，拖累 EUR/USD轉
若，匯價一度下行來至 1.1183，終場收在 1.1202，週線下跌 37點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏空，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區10月 PMI
顯示德、法表現分化，法國製造業及服務業均表現疲弱低於50榮枯線，拖累歐元區
10 月綜合 PMI 由 9 月 53.1 下滑至 51.9，為四個月以來新低，經濟基本面承壓將不
利 EUR/USD上行；此外，法國社會及政經局勢不穩導致法債殖利率突破5%，市場
對其財政及政治危機擔憂持續，也將不利歐元表現。二、美國上週初請失業金人數
降至 19.4萬人，為三個月以來新低，顯示勞動力市場依然穩健，且10月 S&P製造
業 PMI為 56.0續創 53個月新高，服務業及綜合PMI維持穩健，顯示美國經濟仍具
韌性，預期將限縮 EUR/USD上行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線維持空頭排列，且匯價持續承壓於月線之下，顯示短線空方趨勢仍未改
變，在能有效突破上方月線前仍以震盪偏空看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週
呈現震盪偏空的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1120-1.1270

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.19-115.10.22  
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68%  
  公債－10年期- A15109 2.000%~2.002% 
      － 5 年期- A15108     1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，儘管市場預期強烈颱風「薔琵」來襲，週五金融市場可能
因颱風假而暫停交易，銀行間多預留帳上資金以應不時之需，但市況仍屬充
裕，即便有新增調度需求，也很容易滿足，令短率朝區間低位移動。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.68%，利率低位較上週下跌約 1 個基本點。

二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 週四於等殖系統成交在 2.002%，
5 年期指標券 A15108則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.88%。 
週二 10 年期公債標售得標利率 2.001%，落於市場預期區間下緣，並創 2008
年以來新高，顯示市場持續反映經濟成長與通膨預期。 
考量通膨前景及國際債市走勢仍具不確定性，市場觀望氣氛濃厚；惟第四季公
債供給相對有限，可望抑制殖利率大幅彈升。預期短線台債殖利率維持區間整
理，5 年券及 10 年券交易區間分別估於 1.82%~1.84%及 1.98%~2.01%。

## Document 082: 2026年7-9月期GDP予測（1次速報） ～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期GDP予測（1次速報） ～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 10 月30 日

1/3 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） 
～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅ながら４四半期連続のプラス成長を予想、景気回復基調は維持 
11 月16 日に公表される2026 年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋
0.1％）と予測する。４四半期連続のプラス成長になるだろう1。 
物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
一方、設備投資が高い伸びになることが下支えになる見込みである。設備投資の伸びには特殊要
因も一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。個人消費が弱含むなかでも
プラス成長を維持するとみられることは、企業部門の底堅さが景気を下支えしていることを示し
ている。過去３四半期に比較的高い成長が続いた後であることも踏まえれば、今回の減速をもっ
て景気の基調が弱まったと判断する必要はないだろう。家計部門の弱さは懸念材料だが、企業部
門の底堅さは維持されており、景気は引き続き緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
７-９月期のＧＤＰを評価する際には、原油輸入と公的在庫の動きをセットで捉える必要があ
る。４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を
通じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油
が放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt
（前期比年率寄与度▲2.1％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げ
は、公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じるとみられる。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって
原油輸入が回復するため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因になる。一方、輸入の回復に
伴って国家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小す
るとみられる。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.4％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋1.5％
Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し上げることが予想される。

1 10 月 30 日時点で入手可能な経済指標を元に作成している。国際収支や家計調査等、今後公表される経済指標の結果
を踏まえて予測値を修正する可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/3 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げと公的在庫変動による押し上げは、ともに原油の供給環境が改
善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一
体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約するリスクが低下していることである。
ＧＤＰ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用
可能な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むし
ろ前向きに捉えるべきだろう。

需要項目別の動向 
個人消費は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減少を予想する。８～９月に各地で相次いだ大雨や台風などの
天候不順が外出機会やサービス消費を抑制した可能性がある。また、４-６月期には自動車やエア
コンなどの耐久財販売が増加したが、７-９月期にはこうした一時的な押し上げ効果が剥落した。
加えて、物価高による節約志向が根強いなか、食料品や日用品などの非耐久財消費にも弱さがみ
られる。高い賃上げや底堅い雇用環境は下支え要因となっているものの、天候不順という一時的
な要因もあって個人消費は総じて力強さを欠く結果になったとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と、高い伸びを予想する。４-６月期には特許権の海外譲渡に伴う研
究開発投資の減少という特殊要因があったが、７-９月期はその影響が剥落することが押し上げ要
因になった面はある。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を除いても設備投資は明確に増加したと考え
られ、底堅い動きとなっている。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応した省力化
投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資への需要は根強く、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強い。設備投
資計画も高い伸びとなっており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断してよ
いだろう。 
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れとなる見
込みである。両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。 
輸出は前期比＋1.2％と４四半期連続の増加を予想する。４-６月期に大口要因によって研究開
発サービスの受取が急増した分が剥落することで、サービス輸出が減少する一方、財輸出が高い
伸びとなり、財貨・サービス輸出全体では増加を維持したとみられる。半導体製造装置などの資
本財輸出が好調だった。地域別では、ＥＵ向けが低調だった一方、ＡＳＥＡＮ、ＮＩＥｓ向けの
資本財輸出が伸びている。また、４-６月期に急減した中東向け輸出が持ち直したことも押し上げ
につながっている。 
輸入は前期比＋3.3％と大幅な増加が予想される。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が
大幅に減少していたが、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄
与度は前期比▲0.3％Pt（前期比年率▲1.3％Pt）になったと予想される。 
ただし、この外需寄与度のマイナスは、資源輸入の回復に伴う輸入の増加によって生じるもの

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
である。その裏側では公的在庫変動が前期比＋0.4％ポイント、前期比年率では＋1.5％ポイント
の大幅プラス寄与になると見込まれる。両者は併せて評価する必要がある。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.1％Ptと予想する。製品在庫はプラスが予想される一方、流通
在庫の下押しが大きくなるとみられる。 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.9％と、２四半期連続の減少を予想する。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因
となっており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。また、公共投資も同▲1.5％と減少を
予想するほか、政府消費についても、４-６月期の高い伸びの反動から前期比▲0.3％と減少を見
込む。

MPI Corp. (6223.TWO): Richer MEMS mix to drive GM expansion ahead; 
3Q26 beat; TP up to NT$8,600; Buy
12 November 2026 | 12:27AM HKT
Oct revenues on track: Oct revenues came in at NT$2,232mn, +93% YoY and +9% 
MoM (vs. 3Q26 revenues +13% QoQ), accounting for 33% of our 4Q26E revenue 
forecast. Looking into 4Q26, we expect revenue to grow 13% QoQ on continued 
AI-driven demand for both VPC and MEMS probe cards and initial shipments to the 
US CPU customer, and we raise our GM assumption to 62.4% (vs. 60.4% previously) 
to reflect the richer VPC/MEMS mix seen in 3Q26. For 2027, we expect MEMS 
capacity additions, share gains at the US CPU customer and rising pin count per card 
to drive growth, with GM expanding further as MEMS mix rises. 3Q26 beat: Revenue 
was pre-announced. GM of 61.7% came in above GSe/Bloomberg consensus of 
59.8%/59.5%, which we attribute to higher VPC/MEMS shipments for AI-related 
applications. Together with a lower opex ratio of 23.1% (vs. GSe/cons. of 
23.6%/24.1%) on larger revenue scale, operating income grew 26% QoQ, 8.5% / 
11.5% ahead of GSe/cons. EPS of NT$19.18 was 8.1% / 11.0% above GSe/cons. With 
higher GM, we raise our 2026-28E EPS by 5%/3%/1%, and raise our 12m TP to 
NT$8,600 (implied 53x 2027E P/E vs. 90% NI YoY of 2027-28E on avg.). Maintain Buy.

MEMS capacity ramp and new customers to drive growth: Our 4Q26E forecast 
implies average monthly revenue of NT$2,226mn in November-December (flat vs. 
October). On the margin front, we expect product mix to remain favorable for GM, as 
VPC/MEMS capacity additions and new customer ramps continue. Longer term, we 
remain positive on MPI’s growth outlook, driven by 1) MEMS and VPC capacity 
expansion, where we keep our capacity assumption of 2.5mn/8.0mn pins per month 
for VPC/MEMS by end-2027E, which we see as well supported as multiple AI ASIC

and networking programs move into volume; 2) share gains at the US CPU customer, 
where we see potential for it to become MPI’s top revenue contributor in 2027,

and 3.

Earnings revisions: We incorporate the 3Q26 results into our model and revise our 
2026/2027/2028E EPS by +4.8%/+3.2%/+1.2%, as we factor in 1) higher GM, reflecting 
the 3Q26 results and a richer VPC/MEMS mix, and 2) higher revenue 
(+0.8%/+1.2%/+1.5% vs. our prior forecasts). Overall, we now model revenue growth of 
63%/106%/47% YoY over 2026-28E (vs. 61%/105%/46% previously), with GM 
expanding to 60.7%/63.1%/63.9% (vs. 59.6%/62.2%/63.7% previously).

Valuation: As a result of our earnings revisions, we raise our 12-month TP to NT$8,600 
(from NT$8,500). Our 12-month TP is still based on a target P/E of 40x (1.7x of 3-year 
average forward P/E) applied to our 2028E EPS, and discounted back to 2027E at 12.4% 
CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.3x beta (sourcing Bloomberg), (2) a

Price Target Risks and Methodology – MPI (6223.TWO) 
Valuation: We are Buy rated on MPI. Our 12-month TP of NT$8,600 is based on a target 
P/E of 40x (1.7x of 3-year average forward P/E) applied to our 2028E EPS, and 
discounted back to 2027E at 12.4% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.3x 
beta (sourcing Bloomberg), (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 
6.25% (in line with GS house view). 
Key risks to our views: 1) softer AI/HPC demand, 2) slower penetration into new TAM, 
and 3) intensifying competition.

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

ab 11 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
Nan Ya Plastics
Glass fabric capacity expansion to strengthen
vertical integration
What's new
NYP announced plans to invest NT$11.5bn to add 102mn meters of glass fabric capacity
(+17% vs. existing 590mn meters capacity) in Zhangbin, Taiwan, by end-2029.
According to management, the expansion aims to meet growing demand from AI
servers, networking equipment and increasingly complex PCBs, with 60–70% of the

additional capacity allocated for internal use. Capacity will flexibly serve conventional E-
glass and AI-grade specialty glass fabric, with specialty products targeted to reach ~40%
of glass fabric revenue in 2-3 years. NYP is also expanding upstream yarn production and
adding over 500 weaving looms to support its planned glass fabric capacity expansion
by end-2029.
Implications
NYP: Glass fabric expansion to strengthen vertical integration; 1.5–2.0% EM OP

upside from 2030
We view the expansion positively, as glass fabric remains the most supply-constrained
product across NYP's CCL value chain, with the highest OPM of c40% in 3Q26 for glass
fabric. We expect further margin improvement in 4Q26, driven by continued price hikes
and a higher specialty glass fabric mix amid strong AI-related demand. Over the longer
term, the expansion should strengthen NYP's vertical integration and secure upstream
glass fabric supply for downstream CCL production. Assuming full utilization and

current ASP and margins, we estimate the 17% capacity expansion could lift EM OP by
1.5–2.0% from 2030 onwards, with further upside potential from specialty product mix
upgrades.
E-glass fabric: limited impact on external supply
NYP's planned capacity expansion of 102mn meters per year is equivalent to ~2% of
total industry capacity according to our estimates. We expect limited impact on external
E-glass supply, as NYP plans to use 60-70% of output internally, leaving only ~30-40mn

meters annually for external sales before accounting for specialty fabric allocation.
AI-grade specialty fabric: supply constraints likely to persist
We expect supply tightness to persist amid specialty yarn constraints and qualified fabric
production ramp-up. NYP's undisclosed capacity mix further limits visibility on
incremental AI-grade fabric supply.
Valuation: Maintain Buy and PT at NT$390/share
NYP is trading at 10x 27E P/E and 3.5x 27E P/B on our estimates.  Our 26/27E dividend

yields are 3.8%/6.3% at the current share price. We expect limited near-term valuation
impact from the glass fabric expansion, given its expected completion by end-2029.

Company Description
Established in 1958 and listed on the TAIEX in 1967, Nan Ya Plastics one of the core
companies of Taiwan's Formosa Group. Its businesses span electronic materials, chemicals,
plastics processing and polyester. NYP is one of the largest copper clad laminates (CCL)

producers, with a vertically integrated CCL value chain, supplying CCL to its 61%-owned
subsidiary Nan Ya PCB and other printed circuit board (PCB) makers. It owns 29% of Nan Ya
Tech, the only Taiwan-based dynamic random access memory (DRAM) brand. It also owns
23% of Formosa Petrochemical.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Nan Ya Plastics:
We base our price target for NYP on a SOTP valuation.
Key upside risks include: 1) stronger CCL pricing; 2) faster high-end CCL mix upgrades; and 3)

a faster petchem margin recovery.
Key downside risks are: 1) a CCL pricing reversal on faster supply additions or weaker
demand; 2) slower AI qualification/ramp of the company's CCL business; and 3) further
weakening chemicals/polyester businesses.
Nan Ya Printed Circuit Board:
Our price target for Nan Ya Printed Circuit Board is based on P/E multiple methodology.
Upside risks include faster-than-expected ramp-up of networking/switches, stronger-than-

expected pricing increase, better-than-estimated consumer demand, and continued tightness
in BT substrates.
Downside risks include lower-than-expected ramp-up of networking/switch projects, lower-
than-expected global PC/server demand, oversupply from capacity expansions, raw material
cost increases, intensified competition, and a lack of spec upgrades and content increase.
As for industry risks, substrate applications are dominated by PCs, but servers, artificial

intelligence and advanced computing are driving demand growth. We believe key risks to the
industry include slower-than-expected growth in servers, networking, and high performance
computing, ASP pressure, margin erosion, and lack of spec upgrades. We believe the flexible
circuit board and high-density interconnect board segments are more vulnerable to
fluctuations in the consumer product cycle and have lower visibility.
Nanya Technology:
Nanya Tech is a pure-play DRAM manufacturer based in Taiwan mainly focused on legacy DDR

products for customers across various end applications. We think downside risks to our thesis
could include a potential slowdown in end demand and hence the conventional memory
cycle as well as Nanya's legacy-centric product portfolio. We value Nanya Tech shares using a
target NTM P/BV multiple based on long-term average ROE and cost of equity.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

## Document 216: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다실적에 수급을 더한다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다SK하이닉스(000660) 실적에 수급을 더한다실적에 수급을 더한다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
여전히 가격 상승 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 95.8조원, 영업이익 75.1조원으로 예상한다. 
이전 전망 대비 감소한 수준이다. 원/달러 환율 영향이다. 영업이익률은 78.4%로 전 
분기 대비 2.1%p 상승할 전망이다. DRAM 매출액은 73.1조원, 영업이익 
59.2조원으로 추정한다. DRAM ASP는 이전 전망 대비 상향 조정했다. 일부 제품 
가격 인상을 반영한 영향이다. Bit Growth는 이전 전망 수준을 유지했다. ASP 
상향이 수익성 개선에 미친 영향은 크지 않을 전망이다. NAND 매출액은 22.4조원, 
영업이익은 15.9조원으로 예상한다. 
Global Top 수준 주주환원 규모/자사주 살 게 더 있다 
SK하이닉스는 2026년 8월 19일 주주환원에 대한 새로운 정책을 제시했다. FCF에서 
CAPEX를 제외한 금액의 50% 이상을 주주환원에 사용한다는게 핵심이다. 2026년 
기준으로 최소 90조원 수준일 것으로 추정한다. 자사주 중심으로 주주환원을 계획 
중이고 이미 집행 중인 40조원 이외에 4분기 중에 추가로 자사주 매입이 진행될 
것으로 예상한다. 배당도 일부 집행이 되겠지만 자사주가 중심이 될 전망이다. 
흔들리지 말아야 할 이유는 많다 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 모델의 성장으로 메모리에 
대한 수요가 지속적으로 높아지고 있고, 이에 비해 공급 증가는 제한적인 상황이 
상당기간 지속될 것으로 전망하기 때문이다. 이로 인해 현재 수준의 DRAM, NAND 
가격에 변동성은 낮고, 높은 수익성이 유지되면서 안정적 현금흐름 창출이 가능할 
것으로 전망한다. 현재 주가는 지나치게 저평가되어 있는 국면에 있다고 판단한다. 
목표주가는 12개월 예상 EPS 446,092원에 PER 9 배를 적용한 4,000,000원을 
유지한다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

작성자(이선경)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

## Document 251: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기 성장 확대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기_22fa3b02

6개월 목표주가 2,000,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.21) 
1,417,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

## Document 338: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 9-13

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 9-13.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 9-13
Date:    3 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 9-13

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 9-13
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 7 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 2.7% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 4.9%, both
posting their fourth straight week of losses. Thursday’s 4.4% plunge in the
S&P 500, its worst day since April 2025, came as investors weighed what
the Democratic sweep of Congress means for the AI data center buildout.
Both indexes rebounded Friday on strong jobs data, with the S&P 500 up
0.3% and the Nasdaq up 1.4%.
October’s CPI (Tue) and PPI (Fri) headline this week’s data calendar. Ten

Fed officials are scheduled to speak, and the UK and Eurozone report Q3
GDP. Wednesday’s Veterans Day holiday closes the bond market. Here’s
more:
(1) Earnings season. So far, 88% of S&P 500 companies have reported
their Q3 results. So 60 companies still have to report, and 13 will do so
this week. The "Earnings Squiggles" for 2026, 2027, and 2028 are likely to
rise even further over the remainder of this year (chart). Currently, industry
analysts' consensus is that S&P 500 companies’ aggregate EPS will be

$366.60 this year, $426.30 next year, and $495.10 in 2028. In the past,
Earnings Squiggles tended to fall because analysts were too optimistic.
This time, they haven't been optimistic enough!
(2) Fedspeak. Ten Fed officials are scheduled to speak this week
(Goolsbee, Barkin, Waller, Logan, Schmid, Paulson, Hammack, Daly,
Williams, and Jefferson). These are their first remarks after the
Democratic sweep. These Fed heads are likely hawkish on balance,

especially after Friday's strong employment report.

### Page 4

In early October, the federal funds rate (FFR) futures implied 3.3 rate
hikes over the next 12 months and 1.7 over the next six. Since then, those
projections have fallen to 1.9 and 1.4 (chart). Friday’s employment report
lifted the odds of a December rate hike to 78%. We think the FOMC still
needs another 50bps of increases to reach a neutral FFR.
San Francisco Fed President Mary Daly told CNBC on Friday that “the job
market remains stable and solid, but not booming,” with real wages falling

as pay lags inflation. She expects the Fed “will have to tighten policy
further.”
(3) Inflation. October’s CPI and PPI reports will be released on Tuesday
and Friday, respectively. The Cleveland Fed’s Inflation Nowcasting model
projects that the headline CPI rose 0.24% m/m and the core CPI 0.21%
m/m. That would hold headline CPI inflation at 3.7% y/y and ease core to
2.5% from 2.6% (chart). October would mark the 68th consecutive month
of CPI inflation above 2.0% y/y. The model sees both accelerating in

November, to 3.9% and 2.8% y/y.
The Nowcast also suggests that October’s PCED inflation will remain
firmly above the Fed’s 2.0% target, with the headline and core PCED
inflation rates projected at 3.8% y/y and 3.2% y/y, respectively. Both are
expected to heat up a bit in November.

### Page 5

October's PPI inflation is likely to be hotter than September's headline and
core readings of 6.3% and 5.0% (chart).
One of the more troublesome components of the PPI recently has been
transportation & warehousing services, which jumped 16.0% y/y in
September (chart). Soaring diesel prices are fueling inflation.

### Page 6

(4) ADP & unemployment claims. Payrolls rose 165,000 in October, well
above the 65,000 consensus. The unemployment rate fell to 4.1% from
4.2%. ADP’s weekly employment reading (Tue) follows a four-week
average of 16,750 for the week ended October 10, down from 17,500
(chart). ADP’s monthly report showed private payrolls up 86,000 in
October, beating the 67,000 consensus.
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week's 189,000, with the four-week
average falling to 194,750, the lowest since 1969 (chart). Continuing

claims were 1,718,000 for the week ended October 23, with a four-week
average of 1,709,000.

### Page 7

(5) Global. The UK’s Q3 GDP (Thu) rose at an estimated 0.7% q/q pace,
up from 0.5% in Q2, when real GDP was up 1.4% y/y (chart).
The Eurozone’s updated flash estimate of Q3 GDP (Fri) follows the initial
0.4% q/q reading. Real GDP rose 1.3% y/y in Q3, with Spain leading the
major economies at 2.7% and France down 0.1% (chart).

### Page 8

clause.

## Document 346: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

## Document 389: NZ labour market review chartpack, Q3 2026 (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ labour market review chartpack, Q3 2026 (2026-11-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

LABOUR MARKET CHARTPACK
4 Nov 2026

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Key points – September quarter 2026
• The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in labour market conditions. The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an
almost five-year run of increases. The unemployment rate is now at its highest level since September 2015.
• There were mixed messages in terms of jobs growth: the Household Labour Force Survey reported a 0.1% fall in employment, while the employer-

focused Quarterly Employment Survey suggested a modest rise. The Monthly Employment Indicator had also pointed to a small rise in jobs.
• The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in the participation rate. These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly large
increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error in the survey rather than a
genuine surge.
• We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message here is one of softness. Our

best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population.
• While the economy has returned to growth over the last year or so, hiring activity remains weak. This may be an ongoing legacy of employment trends in
previous years, when firms were relatively reluctant to lay people off during the slowdown, which then potentially left them overstaffed even as activity
started to pick up again. It’s not clear how long this effect can persist, though.

• Wage growth remains muted. Although cost-of-living pressures have increased on workers, the existing degree of spare capacity in the labour market
doesn’t give them much leverage to negotiate larger pay increases.
• Overall, we judge these results to be weaker than the RBNZ expected, in particular the rise in the unemployment rate. This data supports the views of
those on the policy committee who were less convinced in October about the potential for second-round inflation pressures in the economy.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
Employment
• The number of people employed fell by 0.1% in the September quarter, according to the
Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS). Previous quarters were revised down a little,
reflecting downward revisions to the working-age population estimates. Annual employment
growth slowed from 0.9% to 0.7%.
• This was substantially softer than our forecast of a 0.5% rise for the quarter. Our forecast
was based on the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI), a comprehensive measure that is

drawn from income tax data. While there are some differences in coverage for these two
measures, any significant divergence between the two is most likely to reflect sampling error
in the HLFS.
• Meanwhile, the employer-focused Quarterly Employment Survey (QES) recorded a 0.1% rise
in the number of filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. (This survey is of course vulnerable
to sampling error as well.)
• We’re most inclined towards the MEI measure of employment, given the breadth of its

coverage. But our best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not
yet fast enough to absorb the growth in the adult population – hence the further rise in the
unemployment rate.

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 4

Westpac New Zealand Economics
4
Labour force participation
• The surprising fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a drop in the participation rate
from 70.6% to 70.4%.
• These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were suggestive of sampling error in the
survey. Any such errors largely wash out when it comes to the unemployment rate, however,
which is why we recommend focusing on this measure.

• Setting aside the usual quarterly volatility, labour force participation remains caught
between two competing forces. One is cyclical weakness, especially among younger people,
who are finding it difficult to break into a “low-fire, low-hire” labour market. The teen
unemployment rate of around 25% is a record high going back to the start of the survey
in 1986.
• The other force is rising participation among the over-60s (who also make up a growing

share of the population). Kiwis have had a growing propensity to work beyond the NZ Super
age, as there is no penalty for doing so. It’s unclear to what extent this can be considered
an increase in the pool of available workers: older workers tend to have low mobility
between jobs, and if they find themselves out of work are more likely to leave the labour
force altogether.

### Page 5

Westpac New Zealand Economics
5
Unemployment and underutilisation
• The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7% in the September quarter. This was the
highest rate since September 2015.
• While there are other indicators of spare capacity in the labour market, we’re not convinced
that there is additional information to draw from them this time.
• The broader underutilisation measure, which includes discouraged jobseekers and part-
time workers wanting more hours, was steady at 13.8% after having risen sharply from

12.9% last quarter. This measure is both higher (by definition) and more variable than the
unemployment rate, but tells a similar story in that it’s around an 11-year high.
• There was a marked 1.6% fall in the number of hours worked (although ‘usual’ hours were
down just 0.4%). However, we suspect this was largely driven by Matariki – a floating holiday
that won’t be captured in the seasonal adjustment.
• This may also account for the 2.5% rise in part-time employment, against a 0.7% fall in

full-time employment. Some workers may have been pushed into the part-time category
(less than 30 hours a week) simply by virtue of only working four days during the
survey week.

### Page 6

Westpac New Zealand Economics
6
Hiring
• Business confidence surveys have shown a lift in hiring intentions in recent months, as firms
have shaken off the initial shock of the Iran conflict and the surge in fuel prices.
• However, those stated intentions aren’t obviously turning into reality yet. Job advertisements
were down about 1% for the September quarter in seasonally adjusted terms, and they
remain well below pre-Covid levels. Job ads will capture ‘churn’ within existing roles as well

as new job creation, but both of these would be associated with a strong labour market.
• With the working-age population growing at the same time, the result was a modest fall in
the vacancy rate, one of the RBNZ’s highest-ranked measures of labour market tightness.
• We estimate that there are around 1.9 people unemployed for every job vacancy, up from
around 1.8 people this time last year.
• Firms have told us that they were reluctant to lay off workers during the economic downturn

in 2023-24, as they were wary of the extreme labour shortages that had developed in the
previous boom years. As a result, they may find themselves still overstaffed even as demand
has started to pick up again, and haven’t needed to return to hiring yet.

### Page 7

Westpac New Zealand Economics
7
Population and migration
• The working-age population grew by around 1.0% over the last year, compared to a peak of
3.3% growth over 2023. Jobs growth (based on the less volatile MEI measure) is still lagging
behind population growth on an annual basis.
• The pool of potential workers is generally becoming older, with the Baby Boomers
increasingly reaching pension age and beyond, and with migrant arrivals tending to be an

older cohort than we saw pre-Covid – more in their 30s and 40s than their 20s.
• Net migration flows have started to pick up from their lows since late 2025. Departures of
New Zealanders have come down from their highs, though they remain above pre-Covid
levels, as the relative appeal of the Australian jobs market has narrowed.
• Foreign arrivals have picked up to some degree, although the growth appears to have been
mostly driven by students rather than those on work visas.

### Page 8

Westpac New Zealand Economics
8
Sectoral and regional trends
• Jobs growth has been relatively strong in government-connected sectors such as healthcare
and education, as well as in local and central government administration.
• Mining and utilities have seen strong growth in percentage terms, but neither of these
sectors are big employers in absolute terms.
• The construction sector is now starting to see a lift in employment, after a steep decline

over 2024-25. Residential building consents have risen strongly over the last year or so,
pointing to a growing pipeline of work for the homebuilding industry.
• Sectors that are connected to international tourism, such as transport and recreational
services, had previously seen strong employment growth, but this appears to have flattened
out in recent months.
• Employment trends have generally been more positive in the South Island than the North,

which likely reflects the greater influence of the agricultural sector – while not a large
employer in itself, farmers have benefited from high export prices and their spending has
flowed into other sectors.

### Page 9

Westpac New Zealand Economics
9
Wages
• The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter. Annual growth
remained around the lowest since 2021.
• While the overall result was in line with our forecast, the details were a little weaker than
we expected, with less contribution from private sector wages (up 0.5%) and a surprisingly
large 0.7% increase in the public sector.
• The distribution of pay rates has returned to pre-Covid trends. Only 44% of roles saw a

pay rise over the last year; of those roles that did see an increase, the average size has
converged on the 2-3% range.
• The LCI is not a measure of what workers actually receive – it reflects the cost to businesses
for a given unit of labour – and hence shouldn’t be compared to the inflation rate. A more
suitable measure is the analytical unadjusted LCI, which includes pay rises that are related
to productivity gains. This measure slowed from 3.1% to 2.9% on an annual basis, the lowest

since March 2021.
• There was a marked pickup in the proportion of firms reporting cost-of-living adjustments as
the main reason for pay increases. However, the size of those increases varied greatly, with
many of them below the rate of CPI inflation.
• This seems to reflect a shift in the justification for pay rises, rather than an overall increase in
wage pressures – firms are more likely to cite cost-of-living adjustments because they’re less
likely to cite demand-side pressures.

### Page 10

Westpac New Zealand Economics
10
International comparisons
• New Zealand’s unemployment rate is the 18th lowest in the OECD (out of 38 countries). This
ranking has been broadly unchanged over the last year or so.
• Notably, New Zealand’s unemployment rate is substantially above Australia’s, whereas
historically it has been lower on average. This shift in relative job opportunities has been a
key attractor for New Zealanders over the last couple of years – as it was in the early 2010s

when the Australian economy was benefiting from a boom in mining construction.
• However, that gap between unemployment rates is no longer widening, with the Australian
jobs market starting to soften this year as the RBA has raised interest rates to rein in inflation
(which was already running too high before the Iran conflict and the surge in oil prices). We
expect Australia’s unemployment to rise further over the year ahead.
• New Zealand has the 7th highest employment rate in the OECD (down from 6th last quarter).

We tend to have high rates of labour force participation for 15-64 year olds, and particularly
high participation for the over-65s, as the lack of means testing for NZ Superannuation
means there is no financial disincentive to working beyond pension age.

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

## Document 454: What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it?
A closer look at the impact of China’s currency on China’s export growth.
Sean Yong/Reuters
UPDATED
October 11, 2026 12:00 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
There is an argument—made in the recent Geneva
Report and by various former IMF officials—that
China’s current export surge is the simple
consequence of the housing downturn and the
resulting weakness in Chinese internal demand.*
The weakness in the RMB in this story is entirely
incidental to the increase in exports, which is, more or
less, a mechanical response to weak internal demand.

Cannot sell excavators at home? Export them.
Built more modern car capacity than your internal
market supports? Export that too…
The fall in the nominal RMB over the course of 2022
and the first half of 2023 in nominal and read terms
was simply a function of domestic macroeconomic
weakness, and a growing rate differential. It wasn’t an
active policy choice by China’s central bank.

### Page 3

3
Now, some might note that China’s currency doesn’t
always move with rate differentials. The RMB didn’t
consistently get pulled up when Chinese rates were
higher than U.S. rates (2002-2020 generally
speaking), and while it did fall when rate differentials
moved against the RMB in 2022 and 2023, it then
stabilized and has even appreciated modestly since
the middle of last year. That appreciation hasn’t been
big enough to correct the deep undervaluation of the

RMB, but it came at a time when the PBOC policy
rate (the reverse repo rate) was well below the U.S.
policy rate.
China doesn’t have an open financial account. As a
result, equilibrium in the foreign exchange market is
thus driven far more by exporter conversion and
current account flows than by rate differentials and
financial flows.** That, together with China’s ongoing
management of the exchange rate and a trade surplus
that can generate significant inflows, means that the

### Page 4

4
value of the RMB is much more of a policy variable
under the independent control of the Chinese
authorities than a simple byproduct of monetary and
fiscal policies.
The modern IMF is reluctant to accept this
conclusion. Its surveillance has generally ignored
China’s currency management (even if that
management is relatively obvious; see the data on FX
settlement) and its recent comprehensive assessment
of China’s economy completely ignored the balance of

payments and exchange rate management.
But it is pretty obvious to the market, even if the
PBOC, rather absurdly, insists that “market forces
play a decisive role in determining the RMB
exchange rate.”
I doubt the PBOC can win this argument. I agree
with Mark Sobel:
“The renminbi is still heavily managed and the
authorities have acted to slow appreciation. The paper
states that the PBoC phased out ‘regular’ foreign
exchange intervention in 2017. But it has many

means at its disposal to achieve the same effect
through extensive capital controls, administrative
guidance, signalling at the fix and deploying state-
owned commercial banks. These means are heavily
used. Thus, the word ‘regular’ must be taken with a
pinch of salt. The comparison to Japan, which

### Page 5

5
intervenes occasionally, is spurious – the yen largely
floats freely in integrated global markets. The same
cannot be said for the renminbi.”
In reality, the PBOC’s daily fix now plays the decisive
role in the currency’s path, and the market is often a
bet on how the PBOC’s management of the yuan will
evolve.
That is clear from the lack of volatility in the yuan’s
rate against the dollar. The yuan’s move down against
the dollar in 2022 was a controlled 10 percent move,

and the appreciation over the last 16 months has been
even more controlled crawl.
And the market is clearly massaged with substantial
help from the state banks. In fact that the pattern of
changes in the monthly FX settlement data looks
exactly like the pattern of intervention of a central
bank managing its currency around a tight band (the

### Page 6

6
state banks in practice buy FX to keep the CNY from
testing the strong side of the band, so there is an
asymmetry in how the CNY is managed)
None of this would matter much if China’s economy
was small. But that obviously isn’t the case. Nor would
this matter much if China’s exports didn’t respond to
changes in the RMB’s real (and nominal) value. But
that is also not the case.
There is abundant evidence that China’s exports do
respond to moves in the currency. Periods of RMB

weakness have consistently been associated with
periods of especially rapid Chinese export growth.

### Page 7

7
Chancellor Merz and President Macron got this
right, even if macroeconomic issues haven’t yet
figured prominently in the EU’s dialogue with
China.*** As they note in their open letter to the
European Commission:
“An appreciation of undervalued currencies towards a value
more in line with fundamentals would help support the
rebalancing of trade accounts with the EU in the short and
medium term … European policy discussions focused on
forging a common understanding of these exchange rate issues

and their interaction with global imbalances would be useful.”
The best evidence that exports responded to changes
in the currency, and not simply to changes in the path
of China’s internal demand, is the acceleration in
China’s export growth after the RMB’s depreciation
over the course of 2022.
This is the killer chart.

### Page 8

8
Now, you might ask: what of the acceleration in
export growth in 2020 and 2021? That was pretty
clearly tied to the global increase in goods demand
that came with the pandemic and work from home
requirements. It was expected to fade when the world
economy normalized. But it didn’t. By 2023 Chinese
export growth again topped global trade growth.
The general expectation that the rise in China’s goods
surplus during Covid would prove to be “transitory”

### Page 9

9
heavily influenced the IMF’s work at the time: see the
IMF’s external sector reports of 2023 and 2024.
I would argue that neglecting the exchange rate was
the key reason the IMF’s kept forecasting, incorrectly,
that China’s current account surplus would fall (See
this blog with Shahin Vallee). The IMF certainly
didn’t warn that China’s property downturn and the
RMB’s real depreciation risked a second China shock
in its 2022, 2023, or 2024 surveillance reports.

Concretely, the weaker RMB appears to have
contributed to a very large outperformance of China’s
exports relative to global trade. See the following
chart
That incidentally also happened after the RMB
followed the dollar down in 2003.
In both periods, there were significant structural
shifts. The WTO accession in 2002, and the success

### Page 10

10
of Chinese industrial policies in the “green” industrial
sectors and autos in 2022. In both cses exports were
turbocharged when structural shifts that should have
resulted in a stronger currency were instead amplified
by currency weakness.
No matter. Paul Krugman has noted that a country
that is converging technologically with its trade
partners should see a trend appreciation in its
currency. China got the technological convergence,
but not the real appreciation—at least not until this

year. And the move since the middle of 2025 has
corrected the depreciation of the RMB that occurred
when the RMB followed the dollar down on
liberation day, but not the structural overvaluation.
Now, there is an argument that exchange rate
adjustment is too hot an issue to make it a priority.
The PBOC isn’t interested it seems.
And some believe that in the interest of comity and
good relations with China, the IMF should mirror
the PBOC’s views. After all a stronger RMB would

slow China’s exports and thus slow China’s economy
at a time when China needs export growth to escape
deflation and generate employment. Or so the
argument goes. Concerns about “external balance“
and the negative global spillovers from China’s rising
goods surplus should be ignored and the Fund’s sole
focus should be on policies that lead to internal

### Page 11

11
balance. That provides one rationale for the Fund’s
ongoing decision to essentially ignore the contentious
issue of the PBOC’s ongoing exchange rate
management.
That argument, however, assumes that China’s
economy is small and thus China’s external surplus
can safely be ignored because it isn’t a drag on the
growth of the IMF’s other members.
That clearly isn’t true.
And it isn’t clear that China is more interested in
having a negotiation over its level of basic pension

benefits or the size of its fiscal deficit (where the
IMF continues to make timid recommendations that
would not move the needle—and certainly wouldn’t
meaningfully reduce China’s external surplus) than a
discussion over its currency practices.****
Back in the world of fixed but adjustable exchange
rates, there was an expectation (not always met to be
sure) that surplus countries would allow their
currencies to strengthen to bring their economies

back into external balance, and that other policy tools
would be used to ensure internal balance.
I would argue that China’s exchange rate (against
both the dollar and the basket) has many similarities
to the old world of fixed but adjustable currencies.
True, it moves on a controlled path when it moves

### Page 12

12
between different levels rather than jumping up and
down. But the PBOC does frequently target a level,
and that targeted level does change over time.
I would further argue that steps like the recent tariff
rate quota that will limit China’s exports of hybrid
cars to Europe to around 350,000 units next year (up
from 2025, but well below the 800,000 units of
2026) aren’t going to address the underlying
imbalance on their own. They can provide a bit of a

buffer for specific sectors.
But that buffer, while necessary, won’t address the
broad-based erosion of manufacturing capacity
outside China. This erosion will persist as long as an
undervalued RMB sends the entire world a signal to
use China as its production base.
The new system of managed trade that is emerging in
autos would need to be progressively expanded to an
ever-increasing set of sectors if protection is used as a
substitute for both domestic reform in China and

exchange rate adjustment.
That is now the direction of travel, but it doesn’t
address the underlying systemic issues.
Incidentally, those who believe the path of internal
demand is critical to rebalancing and that exchange
rate moves are secondary need to explain how an
acceleration in domestic demand would, on its own,

### Page 13

13
lead China’s external accounts to adjust. One
important, stylized fact about China is that domestic
demand growth is no longer feeding over to import
growth (ex-gold, that is).
Bottom line: China, which needs a 1.5 percentage
point contribution from net exports every year, is on a
collision course with its trading partners without a
currency correction that provides the basis for more
balanced trade even in the absence of trade
restrictions.
And there isn’t a realistic path towards balance that

doesn’t involve a stronger RMB; there aren’t, as Paul
Krugman once put it, immaculate transfers.
* That downturn shows up in domestic deflationary
pressure, and weak job growth/high rates of (at least
youth) unemployment. China, in other words, isn’t in
“internal balance.” I really liked the report from

### Page 14

14
Kamakshya Trivedi and Hui Shan of Goldman Sachs
arguing China needs two tools to hit two targets
(internal and external balance).
** To be sure, rate differentials matter for exporter
conversion—but so does the expected movement of
the RMB. The market understands this better than
the Fund. The Chen and Suwanapruti (Goldman
Sachs) note uses a framework similar to mine to
describe the CNY’s moves in Q3:
“In our view, sizeable FX inflows have continued to support

RMB appreciation despite unfavorable interest rate
differentials, as strong export receipts and increased exporter
FX conversions have outweighed portfolio outflows. Our
estimated FX conversion ratio rose to 63% in August,
compared with around 45% in normal years, likely reflecting a
shift in market expectations from RMB depreciation to
appreciation.”
*** Š ef č ovi č  has jurisdiction over trade policy
(narrowly defined), but not broader macroeconomic

policies. Europe’s institutional architecture for
exchange rate coordination is not well-developed.
**** The current account surplus is no longer masked
to the same extent as in 2023 and 2024, as SAFE has
fiddled with its BoP methodology once again (per the
Federal Reserve) and the BoP goods surplus is no
longer significantly below the customs goods surplus.
Of course, there are still problems in the data—the
deficit in the investment income account most

### Page 15

15

## Document 463: 【主要貨幣攻略】政治風險提供歐元逢低布局時機

主要貨幣投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/11/3
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《美國經濟數據表現強勁，支持美元短線維持高點》
美元指數在 9月一波上漲過後呈現高檔盤整 ，10月
指數介於101.5-103內區間盤整。10月初美國公布新
增非農就業僅2.9萬人，疲弱的就業數據令利率市場
大幅降低10月升息預期。然而，美元並未因此而走
弱，10月匯市主軸環繞在歐元區政經風險 。10/5西
班牙宣布提前於 11/29改選，法國財政與政治危機、
中東局勢引發能源價格飆升，法國各區示威行動對經
濟成長帶來隱憂，法國10年期公債殖利率與德國公
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望

美元指數 DXY 99 地緣政治風險未解，通膨帶動升息壓力
歐元 EURUSD 1.19 基本面支持ECB升息，短線政治風險提供逢低布局時機
英鎊 GBPUSD 1.34 英國經濟動能逐漸改善，英國央行維持貨幣緊縮政策
瑞郎 USDCHF 0.8 瑞士總體經濟面有所改善，0利率環境繼續壓抑瑞郎
日圓 USDJPY 158 財政擴張政策與持續刺激措施影響下，市場對債務負擔的疑慮升高。留意短 線政府干預匯市動能

• 萬聖節糖果銷售衰退-6.7%
• 美國製造業PMI創53個月新
高
2
債殖利率利差創 201 1年歐債危機最嚴重時期以來最
高水準，10/22歐盟公布消費者信心指數再度下行 ，
政經不確定性因素令歐元短線承壓。
時序進入 11月份，美國經濟數據表現穩定 ，耐久財
訂單創5個月以來最大單月增幅，加上消費信心連續
4個月走弱後首次回穩 ，10月商諮會消費者信心指
數大幅優於預期 ，分項數據顯示企業經營現況及展
望有所改善。然而，NielsenIQ數據顯示，萬聖節糖
果銷售較去年同期衰退 -6.7%，油價及成本帶動商
品價格上升導致銷售疲弱 。11月可關注美國 11/3期
中選舉過後消費動能是否回升 。另一方面，美國製

造業PMI創53個月新高 ，但也同時顯示價格分項持
續上揚，商品與服務售價創 2022年以來新高，短線
通膨壓力將支持利率維持在歷史相對高點一段時間 ，
預期美元年底前維持高檔震盪格局。

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投

重點摘要
備註:ECB(歐洲央行)
• 法國政治局勢惡化
• 10月PMI對應歐元區GDP季增
率約0.3%
• 歐元年底目標價維持1.19
3
《歐元投資關鍵-法國政治風險令歐元走弱》
10月以來歐元區一連串的動盪導致歐元走弱 。除了
西班牙提前大選 ，法國政治局勢惡化 ，極右派勝出
機率上升加學生示威遊行演變成各地零星暴動 ，經
濟下行風險升溫 。法國政府提出目標將赤字占 GDP
比從5.4%降至5%的方案 ，但反對黨已表態不支持 。
目前執政黨在下議院非多數 ，財政政策停擺風險導
致公債殖利率大幅上升 。法國10年期公債殖利率與
德國公債殖利率利差創2011年歐債危機最嚴重時期
以來最高水準 。本行認為 ，法國右翼已放棄脫歐 ，

且強調財政紀律將遵守歐盟規範 ，法國選後議會仍
需籌組多數聯盟，財政惡化風險可控。
總經方面 ，歐元區經濟基本面持續轉佳 。10月PMI
顯示經濟持續擴張，出口訂單連續第3個月增加，顯
示外需逐步改善 。10月PMI對應歐元區GDP季增率
約0.3%，為近3年相對穩健的季度表現 。通膨方面 ，
能源成本持續上升 ，企業為維持獲利而調升售價 ，
高利率逐漸抑制需求 。德國央行最新通膨報告顯示 ，
目前複製 2021-22年高通膨階段的可能性不大 ，但
通膨風險明顯偏向上行。展望後續，ECB 10月會議
維持利率不變 ，但通膨的僵固性將支持 12月升息，
歐洲與美利差預期小幅收斂 。基本面繼續支持 ECB
升息的環境下 ，預計美歐利差收斂將提供歐元支撐 ，
短線政治風險提供逢低布局時機，4Q26目標價1.19。
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11
市場預期年底前歐元區升息1碼

重點摘要
• 維持英鎊中性評等，英鎊兌美
元年底目標1.34
4
《英鎊投資關鍵-英國經濟動能逐漸改善》
10月經濟數據方面 ，英國PMI初值顯示經濟動能持
續回升。綜合PMI升至53.1，創8個月新高。服務業
商業活動指數升至 53.1，製造業產出指數升至 53.1，
創25個月新高 ，製造業PMI升至52.8。新訂單成長
創2024年9月以來新高 ，帶動企業活動加速擴張 ，
並推動就業人數自 2024年9月以來首度恢復成長 。
然而，投入成本升至6月以來最高水準。主要受能源、
原物料與運輸成本上揚所帶動 ，且中東衝突與供應
鏈干擾延長交貨時間 。企業對一年景氣展望樂觀 ，
但程度仍低於長期平均 。經濟韌性增強及通膨壓力
居高不下 ，使英國央行將維持在貨幣緊縮的路徑 ，

且2027年進一步升息的可能性上升 。對於英鎊的前
景逐漸樂觀，英鎊兌美元年底目標1.34。
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11

重點摘要
• 瑞士PMI連續第7個月擴張
• 4Q26美元兌瑞郎目標價0.8
5
《瑞郎投資關鍵-瑞士景氣連續第7個月擴張》
瑞士9月製造業PMI下滑1.8點至55.3，但連續第7個
月維持在擴張區間。本次下滑主因產出、供應商交貨
時間及就業分項走弱。訂單積壓指數則持穩於57.0，
顯示需求仍具韌性，但企業持續去化庫存，原材料與
成品庫存皆低於50。採購價格指數升至70，反映電
子零組件及原物料價格上漲，中東地緣政治風險及AI
熱潮亦推升成本壓力。服務業PMI由58.3升至59.8，
維持強勁成長，新訂單與商業活動改善，就業指數升
至2025年4月以來高點。採購價格及銷售價格同步明
顯上升，顯示服務業通膨壓力有增溫跡象。整體而言，

瑞士通膨及成長前景溫和，央行並未因地緣政治風險
而強調通膨壓力，利差擴大為瑞郎走弱主因。央行對
升息態度保守，外需回溫與製造業改善正帶動瑞士經
濟展望持續好轉。4Q26美元兌瑞郎維持目標價0.8。

重點摘要
• BOJ10月維持利率不變
• 10/22股市重挫帶動日圓走強
《日圓投資關鍵-股市波動升溫帶動避險情緒》
1個月以來全球科技股明顯回檔 ，美元兌日圓也從
160以上回到158，匯市在美國期中選舉前表現穩定，
日圓小幅偏強但呈區間整理格局。
日本央行在10月份的會議上維持政策不變 。潛在通
膨和中長期通膨預期均已達到2%。日本央行也重申
金融環境依然寬鬆的觀點，這表明與聯準會和歐洲央
行相比，日本央行將更傾向於進一步升息。
10月22日那斯達克當日重挫 -3%帶動日圓一度大幅
走強，但升幅未如7月及9月政府干預匯市之規模 。
短線日圓投機部位已轉為淨多單，市場繼續預期日本
政府對於穩定匯市的決心。財務大臣片山表示，美元

兌日圓匯率較先前接近164時有所改善，當局將對無
序 或 過 度 投 機 行 為 採 取 行 動。 本 行 認 為， 如果
USDJPY升至160以上，日圓買入干預的風險依然很
6

重點摘要
• 美元兌日圓4Q26目標價160
高。
政策方面，近期高市早苗政策面將從 12月起，將汽
油補貼計畫下的基準汽油價格每月提高5日圓，但從
12月起恢復電力和天然氣補貼 。整體而言，隨地緣
政治風險延續，政府控制汽油價格的措施將演變成一
場持久戰，政府將尋求透過動用儲備金和分階段縮減
汽油補貼來減輕財政負擔，但油價長期維持在高點將
繼續對財政帶來壓力。本行認為，日本財政政策及擴
大支出施政方針對日圓不利，惟美元兌日圓160為政
府可容忍的上緣 ，美元兌日圓4Q26目標價維持160。

## Document 512: トルコ中銀は６会合ぶりの利下げも、金融市場の混乱が新たな懸念材料に ～インフレ鈍化で引き締め度合いを調整も、株安によるリ_1298312e

Source PDF: all report pdf/トルコ中銀は６会合ぶりの利下げも、金融市場の混乱が新たな懸念材料に ～インフレ鈍化で引き締め度合いを調整も、株安によるリ_1298312e.pdf

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 23 日

1/3 
トルコ中銀は６会合ぶりの利下げも、金融市場の混乱が新たな懸念材料に 
～インフレ鈍化で引き締め度合いを調整も、株安によるリラ相場や実体経済への波及に警戒～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ トルコ中銀は 10 月 22 日の金融政策委員会において、政策金利(１週間物レポ金利)を 100bp
引き下げ 36.00％とした。利下げは 6 会合ぶりで、金利水準は 2023 年 10 月以来の低さとな
る。背景には、原油高や通貨安への懸念があったにもかかわらず、９月のインフレ率が前年
同月比＋29.73％と 2021 年 11 月以来の低い伸びになるなど、物価の鈍化が着実に進んでい
ることがある。実質金利のプラス幅が拡大して引き締め度合いが強まったため、今回の利下
げは、引き締め姿勢を維持しつつその度合いを調整する従来の動きに沿ったものといえる。 
○ 声明文は過去５会合をほぼ踏襲した。基調的な物価の減速を認める一方、地政学リスクによ
る商品市況の上昇をインフレ見通しの上振れリスクとして警戒し、インフレが著しく悪化し
た場合の再利上げの可能性にも言及しており、「タカ派」姿勢は維持されている。 
○ 金融市場では、 投資信託の流動性を巡る問題に加え、MSCI が 10 月15 日にトルコ株の一部指
数変更を暫定凍結し、2027 年6 月までに市場改革の進展が確認できなければ新興国市場から
フロンティア市場への分類変更を協議する方針を示したことから、主要株価指数は下落基調
にある。市場の混乱が長期化すれば、家計の外貨・金への資産移動を通じたリラ安圧力や、
実体経済・政策運営への悪影響が懸念されるため、今後の動向に注意が必要である。

トルコ中央銀行は、10 月 22 日に開催した定例の金融政策委員会で、政策金利である１週間物レ
ポ金利を 100bp 引き下げて 36.00％とすることを決定した。同行による利下げ実施は６会合ぶりで
あり、政策金利も2023 年 10 月以来の低水準となる。背景には、同国のインフレ率が着実に鈍化し
ていることがある。

### Page 2

2/3 
イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油高が世
界的なエネルギーインフレを招くなか、
原油需要の大部分を輸入に依存する同
国では、その影響が懸念されてきた。さ
らに、原油高は輸入額の増加による対外
収支の悪化を招いており、リラの対ドル
相場はジリ安の展開が続く。そのため、
輸入物価の押し上げもインフレ圧力を
増幅させることが懸念された。しかし、
直近９月のインフレ率は前年同月比＋
29.73％と 2021 年 11 月以来の低い伸び
となった（図１） 。 
同行は 2024 年 12 月に現在の経済チー
ムの下で初の利下げに踏み切り、その後
も実質金利をプラスに維持する抑制的
なスタンスを維持しながら、断続的な利
下げに動いてきた。 このところはインフ
レ率の鈍化を受けて、実質金利のプラス
幅が拡大するなど引き締め度合いが強
まる動きが確認されていた （図２） 。 この
ため、今回の利下げ決定は、引き締め姿
勢を維持しつつ、その度合いを調整して
きた動きに沿ったものと捉えられる。 
会合後に公表した声明文では、 物価動向について 「足元の動きや先行指標は基調的な減速が続い
ていることを裏付けている」との見方を示した。そのうえで「経済指標や供給ショックによる物価
への影響が限定的なものにとどまることは内需の弱さを反映している」 とした。 一方で、 「地政学リ
スクによる商品市況の上昇はインフレ見通しの上振れリスクになる 」として、 「地政学リスクが物
価動向、経済活動、インフレ期待に与える影響を注視する」と、前回会合と同じ見方を示した。 
先行きの政策運営について 「需要動向やリラ相場、 インフレ期待を通じた物価安定を実現するま
で引き締めスタンスを維持する」との従来からの姿勢を維持した。具体的な政策についても「イン
フレ見通しと実績、 基調的な動きを勘案して決定する」 、 「会合ごとにインフレ見通しを注視しつつ
慎重に見直す」 とし、 「インフレ見通しが著しく、 かつ持続的に悪化した場合は引き締め姿勢を強め
る」 、 「インフレの上振れリスクに引き続き高い注意を払っている」と、引き続き再利上げに動く可
能性をあらためて強調した。 
また、 「信用市場や預金市場で予期せぬ事態が発生した場合は、 流動性を巡る状況を注視しつつ、
マクロプルーデンス政策を強化する」 と従来からの考えを繰り返した。 そのうえで、 「インフレ目標

政策決定を行う」との考えをあらためて示している。声明文の内容は、過去５会合をほぼ踏襲して
いるうえ、 過去４会合と同様にインフレの上振れリスクに対する警戒感を強調しており、 「タカ派」

ルコ株式に関する一部の指数変更を暫
定的に凍結し、2027 年６月までに十分な
市場改革の進展が確認できなければ、新
興国市場からフロンティア市場などへ
の分類変更に向けた協議を検討する方
針を示したことがある（注２） 。 こうした
市場の混乱は家計部門のリスク回避姿
勢を強め、外貨や金などへの資産移動を
通じてリラ相場 の下押し圧力につなが
る可能性もある。市場の混乱が長期化すれば、実体経済への悪影響が顕在化するとともに、政府の
政策運営にも影響が及ぶおそれがあり、先行きはその動向に注意を払う必要性が高まっている。

## Document 555: 产业研究报告：中美“AI+制造”的路径选择与全球制造业新秩序

Source PDF: all report pdf/产业研究报告：中美“AI+制造”的路径选择与全球制造业新秩序.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 34
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

产业研究报告
中美“AI+制造”的路径选择与全球制造业新秩序
核心观点 发展研究·产业研究
证券分析师：张立超
0755-81982881
zhanglichao@guosen.com.cn
S0980519050002
相关研究报告
《AI重塑金融投研的范式变革与路径前瞻》 ——2026-09-29
当前人工智能正从通用技术能力加速转化为制造业变革的核心驱动力，“AI+
制造”也由此成为全球主要经济体争夺产业竞争主动权的重要领域。从制造
业的价值链条看，AI渗透主要在研发设计、生产制造、供应链管理和运营管
理等关键节点。“AI+制造”的价值创造逻辑，是以“降本、提质、增效、
创新”为直接表现，以数据、算法、算力构成的新型要素体系为底层支撑，
以组织柔性化和产业生态化为拓展路径，最终推动制造业价值体系实现整体
跃升。
美国发展“AI+制造”的核心战略，是以通用人工智能为长远牵引、以再工
业化为现实抓手、以技术驱动为核心动力，通过政策、资本、技术和规则的
协同发力，谋求在新一轮科技革命和产业变革中重塑制造业竞争优势。依托

人工智能基础层和前沿技术生态布局优势，美国积极在航空航天、半导体设
备、生物医药等高端制造领域寻求突破，特别是AI与增材制造、数字孪生、
先进机器人等前沿技术的交叉融合，旨在通过解决极端复杂工程场景中的
“卡脖子”难题，反向拉动底层技术的迭代升级，从而确保其取得在下一代
制造技术规则制定中的话语权。
中国“AI+制造”的总体战略部署是坚持把发展经济的着力点放在实体经济
上，以应用牵引技术、以场景驱动创新、以全产业链协同释放系统效能，推
动人工智能更好赋能新型工业化。依托全球规模最大、门类最全的制造体系，
中国从投入产出比最清晰、痛点最明确的环节切入，快速形成可复制、可推
广的解决方案，在规模化应用中积累工业数据、打磨算法模型、培育本土供
应商；通过梯次拓展，逐步实现从工具智能、产线智能、工厂智能到产业链
与产业集群智能的层级跃迁，最终以体系化、生态化能力支撑制造业整体迈
向中高端。
人工智能与制造业的深度融合，正在成为重塑全球产业版图的核心驱动力。
全球制造业的竞争格局正在经历“AI+制造”趋势下的深刻重构，主要围绕

以下四个维度展开：一是竞争基础从“成本优势”转向“数据+算法优势”；
二是产业格局从“梯度转移”转向“双向重构”；三是价值链治理从“链式
主导”转向“网络化协同”；四是国际规则从“贸易规则”转向“技术规则
与数据规则”。在此过程中，可关注工业AI基础设施、工业智能体与具身
智能装备、工业软件与关键环节国产替代，以及安全、绿色与服务型制造延
伸带来的相关投资机会。
风险提示：海外市场风险波动；政策效果不及预期。报告列举的公司/个股
仅为案例介绍，不作为投资推荐的依据。
2026年11月11日
